\displaystyle\leq\frac{(K-1)\varepsilon_{t}}{K}\bar{m}+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\langle M_{1}-M_{k},X_{t}\rangle\mathbbm{1}(\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1}-\widehat{M}_{1,t-1},X_{t}\rangle>0)\right]}_{(2)}.

Define
\delta_{t}^{2}
the entrywise bound in Theorem
B.1
such that
\|U_{i,t}V_{i,t}^{\top}-M\|_{\max}^{2}\leq\delta_{t}^{2}
with probability at least
1-8td_{1}^{-200}
, and event
B_{k,t}=\{\langle M_{1}-M_{k},X_{t}\rangle>2\delta_{t}\}
, then the latter expectation can be written as

\displaystyle(2)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\langle M_{1}-M_{k},X_{t}\rangle\mathbbm{1}\left(\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1}-\widehat{M}_{1,t-1},X_{t}\rangle>0\cap B_{k,t}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\langle M_{1}-M_{k},X_{t}\rangle\mathbbm{1}\left(\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1}-\widehat{M}_{1,t-1},X_{t}\rangle>0\cap B_{k,t}^{c}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\bar{m}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}\left(\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1}-\widehat{M}_{1,t-1},X_{t}\rangle>0\cap B_{t}\right)\right]+2\delta_{t}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\mathbbm{1}(B_{k,t}^{c})\right].

For the first term, under event
B_{k,t}
and
\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1}-\widehat{M}_{1,t-1},X_{t}\rangle>0
,

\displaystyle 0>\langle\widehat{M}_{1,t-1}-\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\widehat{M}_{1,t-1}-M_{1},X_{t}\rangle+\langle M_{k}-\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X_{t}\rangle+\langle M_{1}-M_{k},X_{t}\rangle

\displaystyle>\langle\widehat{M}_{1,t-1}-M_{1},X_{t}\rangle+\langle M_{k}-\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X_{t}\rangle+2\delta_{t}.

This means either
\langle\widehat{M}_{1,t-1}-M_{1},X_{t}\rangle<-\delta_{t}
or
\langle M_{k}-\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X_{t}\rangle<-\delta_{t}
, which further implies either
\|\widehat{M}_{1,t-1}-M_{1}\|_{\max}
or
\|M_{k}-\widehat{M}_{k,t-1}\|_{\max}
should be larger than
\delta_{t}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}(\langle{M}_{k,t-1}-{M}_{1,t-1},X_{t}\rangle>0)\cap B_{t}\right]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}\left(\langle{M}_{k,t-1}-{M}_{1,t-1},X_{t}\rangle>0\cap B_{k,t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|{M}_{1,t-1}-M_{1}\|_{\max}>\delta_{t}\right)+{\mathbb{P}}\left(\|{M}_{k,t-1}-M_{k}\|_{\max}>\delta_{t}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{16(t-1)}{d^{200}},

For the other term, since
{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}(B_{k,t}^{c})\right]={\mathbb{P}}(\langle M_{1}-M_{k},X_{t}\rangle<2\delta_{t})\leq 1
, then

\displaystyle r_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon_{t}\bar{m}+\frac{16(K-1)(t-1)}{d^{200}}\bar{m}+2(K-1)\delta_{t}.

The total regret up to time
T
is

\displaystyle R_{T}

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=1}^{T}r_{t}\leq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\varepsilon_{t}\bar{m}+\frac{8KT(T-1)}{d_{1}^{200}}\bar{m}+2K\sum_{t=1}^{T}\delta_{t}.

Since
\delta_{t}^{2}=C_{3}\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{2}r^{3}}{d_{1}d_{2}}\prod_{\tau=1}^{t}(1-\frac{c_{1}\log d_{1}}{4\kappa T^{1-\gamma}})+C_{4}\frac{t\cdot rd_{1}\log^{4}d_{1}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{T^{2(1-\gamma)}}
when
1\leq t\leq T_{0}
and
\delta_{t}^{2}=C_{5}\frac{rd_{1}\log^{4}d_{1}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{T^{1-\gamma}}
when
T_{0}+1\leq t\leq T
, for some constants
C_{3}
,
C_{4}
and
C_{5}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle R_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{t=1}^{T_{0}}\varepsilon\bar{m}+\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{1}{t^{\gamma}}\bar{m}+K\frac{\bar{m}T^{2}}{d_{1}^{200}}

\displaystyle\quad+K\sum_{t=1}^{T_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{\lambda_{\min}^{2}r^{3}}{d_{1}d_{2}}\prod_{\tau=1}^{t}(1-\frac{c_{1}\log d_{1}}{4\kappa T^{1-\gamma}})}+K\sum_{t=1}^{T_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{t\cdot rd_{1}\log^{4}d_{1}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{T^{2(1-\gamma)}}}+K\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sqrt{\frac{rd_{1}\log^{4}d_{1}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}{T^{1-\gamma}}}.

Since
T\ll d_{1}^{50}
, the third term is negligible. As a result, from the fact that
\sum_{t=1}^{T}t^{-\gamma}\lesssim T^{1-\gamma}
,

\displaystyle R_{T}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{1-\gamma}\bar{m}+KT^{1-\gamma}\frac{\lambda_{\min}r^{3/2}}{\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}}+KT^{(1+\gamma)/2}\sqrt{rd_{1}\log^{4}d_{1}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{1-\gamma}\bar{m}+KT^{(1+\gamma)/2}\sqrt{rd_{1}\log^{4}d_{1}\bar{\sigma}^{2}}.

∎

D.12
Proof of Theorem
B.3

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we prove
a=1
case and omit all the subscript. Recall that
\langle\widehat{M},Q\rangle-\langle M,Q\rangle
can be decomposed into

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{M},Q\rangle-\langle M,Q\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle LL^{\top}\widehat{Z}_{1}R_{\perp}R_{\perp}^{\top},Q\rangle+\langle L_{\perp}L_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{Z}_{1}RR^{\top},Q\rangle+\langle LL^{\top}\widehat{Z}_{1}RR^{\top},Q\rangle

(17)

\displaystyle\quad+\langle LL^{\top}\widehat{Z}_{2}R_{\perp}R_{\perp}^{\top},Q\rangle+\langle L_{\perp}L_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{Z}_{2}RR^{\top},Q\rangle+\langle LL^{\top}\widehat{Z}_{2}RR^{\top},Q\rangle

(18)

\displaystyle\quad+\langle\widehat{L}\widehat{L}^{\top}\widehat{Z}\widehat{R}\widehat{R}^{\top},Q\rangle-\langle LL^{\top}\widehat{Z}RR^{\top},Q\rangle

(19)

\displaystyle\quad+\langle\sum_{k=2}^{\infty}(\mathcal{S}_{A,k}(\widehat{E})A\Theta\Theta^{\top}+\Theta\Theta^{\top}A\mathcal{S}_{A,k}(\widehat{E})),\widetilde{Q}\rangle+\langle(\widehat{\Theta}\widehat{\Theta}^{\top}-\Theta\Theta^{\top})A(\widehat{\Theta}\widehat{\Theta}^{\top}-\Theta\Theta^{\top}),\widetilde{Q}\rangle,

(20)

where
\widehat{Z}_{1}=\frac{b}{T}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{d_{1}d_{2}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t}=1)}{\pi_{t}}\xi_{t}X_{t}
and
\widehat{Z}_{2}=\frac{b}{T}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\left(\frac{d_{1}d_{2}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t}=1)}{\pi_{t}}\langle\widehat{\Delta}_{t-1},X_{t}\rangle X_{t}-\widehat{\Delta}_{t-1}\right)
. The only different analysis is the main term (
17
). (
18
)-(
20
) can be shown negligible following the same arguments in Lemma
5
-
7
. Next we show the asymptotic normality of (
17
).

Denote
S^{2}=1/T^{\gamma}\|\mathcal{P}_{\Omega_{1}}(\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q))\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}+C_{\gamma}\|\mathcal{P}_{\cup_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\Omega_{k}}(\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q))\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}
. By definition,

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{\frac{T^{1-\gamma}}{d_{1}d_{2}}}\left(\langle LL^{\top}\widehat{Z}_{1}R_{\perp}R_{\perp}^{\top},Q\rangle+\langle L_{\perp}L_{\perp}^{\top}\widehat{Z}_{1}RR^{\top},Q\rangle+\langle LL^{\top}\widehat{Z}_{1}RR^{\top},Q\rangle\right)}{\sigma S}

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\sqrt{\frac{1}{T^{1+\gamma}}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{b\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t}=1)}{\pi_{t}}\xi_{t}\langle X_{t},\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle}{\sigma S}.

Similarly, we apply Theorem 3.2 and Corollary 3.1 in
Hall and Heyde [2014]
, the Martingale Central Limit Theorem to show the asymptotic normality.

Step 1: checking Lindeberg condition.

For any
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{b^{2}d_{1}d_{2}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t}=1)}{\sigma^{2}S^{2}T^{1+\gamma}\pi_{t}^{2}}\xi_{t}^{2}\langle X_{t},\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}\times\mathbbm{1}\left(\left|\xi_{t}\frac{\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t}=1)\langle X_{t},\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle}{\sigma S\sqrt{T^{1+\gamma}}\pi_{t}}\right|>\delta\right)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b^{2}d_{1}d_{2}}{\sigma^{2}S^{2}T^{1+\gamma}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\mathbbm{1}(a_{t}=1)}{\pi_{t}^{2}}\xi_{t}^{2}\langle X_{t},\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}\times\mathbbm{1}\left(\left|\xi_{t}\langle X_{t},\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle\right|>\frac{\sigma S\delta\sqrt{T^{1+\gamma}}\varepsilon_{t}}{2\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}}\right)\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{b^{2}d_{1}d_{2}}{\sigma^{2}S^{2}T^{1+\gamma}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{t}}\max_{X\in\mathcal{X}}\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}\times\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}\left(\left|\xi_{t}\langle X_{t},\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle\right|>\frac{\sigma S\delta\sqrt{T^{1+\gamma}}\varepsilon_{t}}{2\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}}\right)\right]},

where in the last inequality we use
{\mathbb{E}}[XY]\leq\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}[X^{2}]{\mathbb{E}}[Y^{2}]}
.
By incoherence condition,

\displaystyle\max_{X\in\mathcal{X}}\big|\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle\big|\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d_{2}}}\|\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\|_{\rm{F}}.

Moreover, since
\xi_{t}
is a subGaussian random variable, so the product
\xi_{t}\langle X_{t},\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle
has subGaussian tail probability

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\left|\xi_{t}\langle X_{t},\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle\right|>\frac{\sigma S\delta\sqrt{T^{1+\gamma}}\varepsilon_{t}}{2\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}}}\right)<2e^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}S^{2}\delta^{2}T^{1+\gamma}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}}{8d_{1}d_{2}\nu^{2}}},

where
\nu
is the subGaussian parameter of order
O(\sqrt{\frac{\mu r}{d_{2}}}\|\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\|_{\rm{F}}\sigma)
. Notice that
T^{1+\gamma}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}\leq T^{1+\gamma}\varepsilon_{T}^{2}=O(T^{1-\gamma})
. By property of exponential function,
\frac{d_{1}d_{2}\sigma^{2}}{\sigma^{2}S^{2}T^{1+\gamma}}\frac{\mu r}{d_{2}}\|\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{2}{\varepsilon_{t}}2e^{-\frac{\sigma^{2}S^{2}\delta^{2}T^{1+\gamma}\varepsilon_{t}^{2}}{16d_{1}d_{2}\nu^{2}}}
converges to 0 as long as
\frac{d_{1}}{T^{1-\gamma}}\rightarrow 0
. Then the Lindeberg condition is satisfied.

Step 2: calculating the variance

Next, we show the conditional variance equals to 1. Recall the definition
\Omega_{1}=\{X\in\mathcal{X}:\langle M_{1}-M_{0},X\rangle>\delta_{T,d_{1},d_{2}}\}
,
\Omega_{0}=\{X\in\mathcal{X}:\langle M_{1}-M_{0},X\rangle<\delta_{T,d_{1},d_{2}}\}
and
\Omega_{\emptyset}=\mathcal{X}\cup(\Omega_{1}\cup\Omega_{0})^{c}
. Then

\displaystyle\quad\frac{b^{2}d_{1}d_{2}}{\sigma^{2}S^{2}T^{1+\gamma}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\mathbbm{1}(a_{t}=1)}{\pi_{t}^{2}}\xi_{t}^{2}\langle X_{t},\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}\bigg|\mathcal{F}_{t-1}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{b^{2}}{\sigma^{2}S^{2}T^{1+\gamma}}\sigma^{2}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{X\in\Omega_{1}}\mathbf{1}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle=1\right)\frac{1}{1-\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{K}}\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+\mathbf{1}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle\neq 1\right)\frac{1}{\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{K}}\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\sum_{X\in\Omega_{k}}\mathbf{1}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle=1\right)\frac{1}{1-\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{K}}\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}+\mathbf{1}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle\neq 1\right)\frac{1}{\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{K}}\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{X\in\Omega_{\emptyset}}\mathbf{1}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle=1\right)\frac{1}{1-\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{K}}\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}+\mathbf{1}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle\neq 1\right)\frac{1}{\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{K}}\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}.

We aim to show the convergence of the indicator function. For any
X\in\cup_{k\in[K]}\Omega_{k}
and any
j,w\in[K]
, suppose
\langle M_{j}-M_{w},X\rangle>0
, we have for any
T_{0}+1\leq t\leq T
,

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{M}_{j,t}-\widehat{M}_{w,t},X\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\widehat{M}_{j,t}-M_{j},X\rangle+\langle M_{w}-\widehat{M}_{w,t},X\rangle+\langle M_{j}-M_{w},X\rangle

\displaystyle\geq\langle M_{j}-M_{w},X\rangle-2\delta>0

with probability at least
1-8td_{1}^{-200}
, where
\delta
is defined in Theorem
B.3
. The second inequality comes from the gap condition in Assumption
B.1
. Similarly, if
\langle M_{j}-M_{w},X\rangle<0
, we can show
\langle\widehat{M}_{j,t}-\widehat{M}_{w,t},X\rangle<0
with the same probability. As a result, with the same probability,
\mathbbm{1}(\langle\widehat{M}_{j,t}-\widehat{M}_{w,T},X\rangle>0)=\mathbbm{1}(\langle M_{j}-M_{w},X\rangle>0)
. Combine with a union bound, with probability at least
1-8Ktd_{1}^{-200}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle=1\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\mathbf{1}\left(\langle\widehat{M}_{1,t}-\widehat{M}_{k,t},X\rangle>0\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\mathbf{1}\left(\langle{M}_{1,t}-{M}_{k,t},X\rangle>0\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{1}\left(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle{M}_{k},X\rangle=1\right).

Therefore,
\sum_{X\in\Omega_{1}}\mathbf{1}(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle=1)\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}\rightarrow\|P_{\Omega_{1}}(P_{M}(Q))\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}
and

\sum_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\sum_{X\in\Omega_{k}}\mathbf{1}(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle\neq 1)\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}\rightarrow\|P_{\cup_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\Omega_{k}}(P_{M}(Q))\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}
. On the other hand,
\sum_{X\in\Omega_{1}}\mathbf{1}(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle\neq 1)\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}\rightarrow 0
and

\sum_{k\in[K]\backslash\{1\}}\sum_{X\in\Omega_{k}}\mathbf{1}(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle=1)\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}\rightarrow 0
.
Also note that
\frac{b}{T}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}1/(1-\varepsilon_{t}/K)\rightarrow 1
and
\frac{b}{T^{1+\gamma}}\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}K/\varepsilon_{t}\rightarrow\frac{K}{c_{2}(1+\gamma)}
. And by Assumption
B.1
,
\sum_{X\in\Omega_{\emptyset}}\mathbf{1}(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle=1)\frac{1}{1-\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{K}}\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}+\mathbf{1}(\max_{k\in[K]}\langle\widehat{M}_{k,t-1},X\rangle\neq 1)\frac{1}{\frac{\varepsilon_{t}}{K}}\langle X,\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle^{2}\leq K/\varepsilon_{t}\|{\mathcal{P}}_{\Omega_{\emptyset}}({\mathcal{P}}_{M}(Q))\|_{\rm{F}}^{2}
is negligible. Then the conditional variance will converge in probability to 1. It follows

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{d_{1}d_{2}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t}=1)}{\pi_{t}}\xi_{t}\langle X_{t},\mathcal{P}_{M}(Q)\rangle}{\sigma S\sqrt{d_{1}d_{2}/T^{1-\gamma}}}\rightarrow N(0,1)

when
T,d_{1},d_{2}\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

D.13
Proof of Corollary
B.1

Proof.

Note that for any
g,h\in[K]

\displaystyle(\langle\widehat{M}_{g},Q\rangle-\langle\widehat{M}_{h},Q\rangle)-(\langle M_{g},Q\rangle-\langle M_{h},Q\rangle)=(\langle\widehat{M}_{g},Q\rangle-\langle M_{g},Q\rangle)-(\langle\widehat{M}_{h},Q\rangle-\langle M_{h},Q\rangle),

then
\langle\widehat{M}_{g},Q\rangle-\langle M_{g},Q\rangle
and
\langle\widehat{M}_{h},Q\rangle-\langle M_{h},Q\rangle
can be decomposed into main term and negligible terms in the same way as Theorem
3
. The upper bound of all the negligible terms follow the Lemma
5
-
7
. The main CLT term is

\displaystyle\frac{b}{T}\sum_{t_{1}=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{d_{1}d_{2}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{1}}=g)}{\pi_{g,t_{1}}}\xi_{t_{1}}\langle X_{t_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{M_{1}}(Q)\rangle-\frac{b}{T}\sum_{t_{2}=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{d_{1}d_{2}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{2}}=h)}{\pi_{h,t_{2}}}\xi_{t_{2}}\langle X_{t_{2}},\mathcal{P}_{M_{0}}(Q)\rangle.

As long as we show
\sum_{t_{1}=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{d_{1}d_{2}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{1}}=g)}{\pi_{g,t_{1}}}\xi_{t_{1}}\langle X_{t_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{M_{g}}(Q)\rangle
is uncorrelated with

\sum_{t_{2}=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{d_{1}d_{2}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{2}}=h)}{\pi_{h,t_{2}}}\xi_{t_{2}}\langle X_{t_{2}},\mathcal{P}_{M_{h}}(Q)\rangle
, then the asymptotic variance is the sum of their individual variance, i.e.,
\sigma_{g}^{2}S_{g}^{2}+\sigma_{h}^{2}S_{h}^{2}
. Notice that,

\displaystyle\quad\frac{b^{2}}{T^{2}}\sum_{t_{1}=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{d_{1}d_{2}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{1}}=g)}{\pi_{g,t_{1}}}\xi_{t_{1}}\langle X_{t_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{M_{g}}(Q)\rangle\sum_{t_{2}=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{d_{1}d_{2}\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{2}}=h)}{\pi_{h,t_{2}}}\xi_{t_{2}}\langle X_{t_{2}},\mathcal{P}_{M_{h}}(Q)\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{b^{2}d_{1}^{2}d_{2}^{2}}{T^{2}}\sum_{t_{1}=T_{0}+1}^{T}\sum_{t_{2}=T_{0}+1}^{T}\frac{\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{1}}=g)\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{2}}=h)}{\pi_{g,t_{1}}\pi_{h,t_{2}}}\xi_{t_{1}}\xi_{t_{2}}\langle X_{t_{1}},\mathcal{P}_{M_{g}}(Q)\rangle\langle X_{t_{2}},\mathcal{P}_{M_{h}}(Q)\rangle.

When
t_{1}=t_{2}
,
\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{1}}=g)\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{2}}=h)=0
. When
t_{1}\neq t_{2}
,
{\mathbb{E}}[\frac{\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{1}}=g)\mathbbm{1}(a_{t_{2}}=h)}{\pi_{g,t_{1}}\pi_{h,t_{2}}}\xi_{t_{1}}\xi_{t_{2}}X_{t_{1}}X_{t_{2}}]=0
due to the i.i.d. distributed
\xi_{t}
. As a result, the two terms are uncorrelated.

Together with Lemma
5
-
7
, all the negligible terms converge to 0 when divided by

\sqrt{(\sigma_{g}^{2}S_{g}^{2}+\sigma_{h}^{2}S_{h}^{2})d_{1}d_{2}/T^{1-\gamma}}
. Then similar as Theorem
4
, we can replace
\sigma_{g}^{2}S_{g}^{2}+\sigma_{h}^{2}S_{h}^{2}
with
\widehat{\sigma}_{g}^{2}\widehat{S}_{g}^{2}+\widehat{\sigma}_{h}^{2}\widehat{S}_{h}^{2}
and conclude the proof.
∎
